# Stringbean sbx

Use this skill to invoke Stringbean from inside Codex.

## Behavior

1. Convert the user's request into the exact task text for Stringbean.
2. Run Stringbean with full plugin output enabled:

```bash
sbx "<task and flags>" --plugin-full-output
```

If the current working directory is the Stringbean repository and the repo-local wrapper exists,
this equivalent command is also acceptable:

```bash
plugins/stringbean/scripts/sbx-codex "<task and flags>"
```

3. Preserve user-specified Stringbean flags such as `--rw`, `--ro`, `--mode auto`,
   `--mode low`, `--mode medium`, and `--mode high`.
4. Do not add a permissions flag unless the user asks for one. Stringbean's default profile is
   `rw`; use `--ro` only when the user explicitly asks for create-only/read-only behavior.
5. Show useful live Stringbean output while the command runs. Lines beginning with
   `STRINGBEAN_INTERMEDIATE:` are progress/status lines only, not final output.
   Use a terminal timeout of at least 1,800 seconds when the tool supports one. If the
   terminal yields a running session, poll it every 5-10 seconds and do not terminate it
   while fresh Stringbean heartbeat or agent-output lines continue to arrive.
6. After the command finishes, find the final block between:

```text
STRINGBEAN_FINAL_START
STRINGBEAN_FINAL_END
```

Inside it, keep backward compatibility by reading the result block between:

```text
STRINGBEAN_RESULT_START
STRINGBEAN_RESULT_END
```

7. The visible final answer must report the useful fields from that block, especially:
   `Status`, `Result`, `Tasks`, `Review rounds`, and `Artifacts`.

## During the run

Stringbean emits normal visible run output and explicitly marked progress lines before the final block:

```text
STRINGBEAN_INTERMEDIATE: Progress: ...
STRINGBEAN_INTERMEDIATE: Agent: ...
STRINGBEAN_INTERMEDIATE: Agent output: ...
```

Plugin wrappers request a heartbeat every five seconds by default. A live heartbeat means
the command is active, even when the provider is still waiting for its next model response.
Each provider subprocess launch also emits an explicit `STRINGBEAN_INTERMEDIATE: Command:` line.

Use those lines for brief user-facing updates if the run takes time. These lines are already
sanitized: they describe phases, selected agents, parsed summaries, verdicts, bounded
still-running heartbeats, visible assistant messages, tool calls, and capped tool output. They do
not include hidden chain-of-thought or raw transcripts. Do not confuse them with final output, and
do not invent generic progress text when a specific `STRINGBEAN_INTERMEDIATE:` line is available.

## Output rules

- Prefer the visible final result block over raw logs.
- Do not tell the user to press Ctrl+T to see the result.
- Do not expose hidden chain-of-thought. Progress lines are observable status, not reasoning.
- Keep the final answer short and focused on Stringbean's final result.
- If Stringbean fails, report the concise failure reason and any artifact path that was printed.

labeled as documented features):**\n\nCore capabilities explicitly listed:\n- Open SSH terminal sessions\n- Browse and transfer files with SFTP\n- Copy files with SCP\n- Save hosts for one-tap reuse\n- Manage SSH identities and private keys\n- Set up local port forwards\n- Share or import hosts, identities, and forwards with QR codes\n- Save snippets for repeated commands\n\nAdditional documented features and UI areas (from user wiki docs):\n- Home screen: landing with open sessions (when active), favorites (grouped by hosts/identities/port forwards/snippets), mixed recents list; welcome/add buttons when no resources saved yet.\n- Quick Connect: for ad-hoc/one-time connections via drawer or button.\n- Hosts management: add via form (name, host/IP, port, username, auth method (password/identity/both), optional group/notes/startup snippet/default local forward/terminal profile/transport (SSH or Mosh)/background behavior), edit, delete, groups (collapsible), search, sort (Last Used or Alphabetical), launch SSH/SFTP/SCP/Info/QR from card; import from OpenSSH config file.\n- Identities: add/import private keys (paste or file), manage fingerprints, QR share/import (with export passphrase option for sensitive), groups, favorites, edit/delete.\n- Port Forwards: local forwards only (-L documented), add/edit (bind/dest fields), enable/disable, associate with hosts for auto-start on connect, QR share/import, groups, favorites.\n- Snippets: store reusable commands/scripts; add/edit/delete/import via QR; run against active session(s) (documented as first active session); groups.\n- Keyboard Editor: customize compact terminal key row (assign keys, modifiers, icons).\n- Theme Editor: manage terminal profiles (font size, colors, cursor); duplicate built-ins or create custom; hosts fall back on delete.\n- Settings: app theme (System/Light/Dark), background session toggle, terminal emulation (xterm or vt100), selection mode (Natural/Block), security (Set PIN, biometric unlock after PIN, lock timeout), host key prompt controls (keep enabled, auto-trust option), port forward auto-start, diagnostics/privacy, restore defaults, Transfer Data (Export via QR, with options to include identities/settings).\n- Security notes (documented): review host key fingerprints before trusting; PIN + biometric for extra protection; export passphrase for QR payloads containing passwords or private keys.\n- Other: In-app drawer navigation to all areas + Help (in-app guide + external support link) and About (version, links, licenses). Import actions in top app bar for management screens. Grouped/collapsible sections with three-dot menus for actions on cards.\n\n**Notes on labeling and scope:**\n- All above are presented as documented claims from README and user/developer wiki content.\n- Developer docs (Product-Blueprint.md, UI-Design-Notes.md, Developer-Home.md) describe vision, target users (e.g. SRE, indie dev, IT consultant), constraints (Android 8+, Kotlin/Compose, offline-first QR sync, no Telnet/local shell), more detailed feature expectations (e.g. dual-pane SFTP, OS/distro icon detection from uname, info panels with editable command presets, multi-frame QR, specific keyboard behaviors), architecture notes (Jetpack Compose, Room, sshj, etc.), and future enhancements (discovery integrations, etc.). docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:1:# SSHPeaches Troubleshooting
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:12:If the app asks for password repeatedly, verify server-side SSH settings (`PasswordAuthentication`, `PubkeyAuthentication`, and allowed users).
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:16:If SSHPeaches reports a changed host key:
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:26:- App is locked (unlock first).
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:28:- Wrong passphrase when importing encrypted QR key payload.
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:30:Try importing a standard OpenSSH private key file again from **Identities**.
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:32:## QR import says duplicate or invalid
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:34:- Host QR import rejects duplicates by host name.
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:35:- Identity QR may request overwrite if fingerprint already exists.
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:36:- Port forward import supports only local forwarding type.
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:44:- Re-run snippet from **Snippets**.
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:47:## SFTP/SCP transfer issues
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:66:- Use **Unlock with PIN** on lock screen.
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:67:- If biometric is configured and available, use biometric unlock.
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:72:QR transfer supports:
docs/user-wiki/Troubleshooting.md:76:- Port forwards

docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:1:# SSHPeaches User Guide
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:3:This document explains each major screen in SSHPeaches 0.1.x.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:27:- Shows favorites grouped by type: hosts, identities, port forwards, and snippets.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:29:- If the app has no saved resources yet, Home shows a welcome screen with buttons to add your first host, identity, port forward, or snippet.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:41:- Use the top app bar icons to add a host, scan a host QR, or import hosts from an OpenSSH config file.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:46:- `SSH` starts terminal mode.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:47:- `SFTP` opens SFTP browser mode.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:49:- `Info` manages/runs info snippets for that host.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:50:- `QR` shares host config.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:52:If a host has a saved password, QR export prompts for a passphrase and encrypts that password payload.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:58:- In the add/edit form, the authentication block sits directly below `Username`
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:59:- Optional: group, notes, transport (SSH/Mosh), terminal profile, preferred local forward, startup snippet, background behavior
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:65:- `SSH`: terminal view with compact custom key row
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:66:- `SFTP`: file browsing and upload/download controls
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:77:Use identities for SSH key authentication.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:81:- Generate/share identity QR
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:82:- Import identity via QR
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:87:When exporting an identity with a private key, SSHPeaches requires an export passphrase and encrypts key data inside the QR payload.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:91:SSHPeaches currently supports local forwards only (`-L`).

docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:94:- Enable/disable a forward

docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:98:- Organize forwards into collapsible groups
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:101:Remote (`-R`) and dynamic (`-D`) forwarding are not in the current app scope.
docs/user-wiki/User-Guide.md:105:Use snippets for reusable shell commands.

docs/user-wiki/developer/UI-Design-Notes.md:1:# SSHPeaches UI Design Notes
docs/user-wiki/developer/UI-Design-Notes.md:5:This document captures first-pass layouts for key SSHPeaches screens. Use it as a blueprint when building mockups (Figma/Penpot) and implementing Compose UI.

docs/user-wiki/developer/UI-Design-Notes.md:39:  - Remote (`-R`) and Dynamic (`-D`) forwarding are intentionally out of scope.
docs/user-wiki/developer/UI-Design-Notes.md:40:  - These are SSH TCP forwards, not VPN; only apps pointed at the bind port use them.

docs/user-wiki/developer/UI-Design-Notes.md:131:- During sessions, toolbar indicator shows active forwards with toggles/status badges; disabling stops the tunnel but keeps SSH session alive. Toast/notification confirms success/failure.
docs/user-wiki/developer/UI-Design-Notes.md:148:- Users drag from palette to any row; keys behave like movable blocks within each row (free placement along the row's width, no strict grid).

docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:1:# SSHPeaches Product Blueprint
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:6:- Provide a trustworthy, modern Android-phone/tablet SSH/Mosh client that feels tailored for touch interaction yet powerful enough for professional administrators.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:7:- Ship a Play Store-ready app within 6-8 months, launching as *SSHPeaches* with all features available for free at launch.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:12:2. **Indie developer ("Leo")** - values customization, fast remote access, simple offline backups (QR export, USB).
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:17:- Respect user security: private keys never leave device unencrypted; biometric unlock required for sensitive actions.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:18:- Offline-first: core SSH must work without network services; QR-based peer sync avoids recurring cloud costs.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:20:- Scope boundary: no Telnet or on-device local shell modes to maintain focus on SSH/SFTP/SCP/Mosh.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:23:- Home is the default landing view. It combines open sessions, favorites grouped by type, and a mixed recents feed across hosts, identities, port forwards, and snippets. If the app is empty, Home shows a welcome panel with direct add actions for each resource type. Hamburger menu opens the rest of the app (Quick Connect, Hosts, Identities, Port Forwards, Snippets, Keyboard Editor, Settings, Help, About). Help launches the official support website; About opens an in-app sheet with logo, version, build info, and links to licenses.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:24:- Multi-mode sessions: per-host quick-launch icon buttons (large icon + label such as "SSH", "SFTP", "SCP", "Info", "Move") covering terminal, full two-way SFTP browser (FileZilla-style), SCP "Quick Transfer", and informational panels; supports multiple simultaneous sessions, reconnect, keep-alive, and agent forwarding (where possible via ssh-agent emulation).
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:25:- SFTP explorer: dual-pane (local/remote) view with drag-to-queue transfers, resumable progress, bookmarks, and transfer history.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:27:- Connection manager: grouped sections, search, favorites, quick actions, import/export (JSON/URI), inline icon buttons (SSH, SFTP, SCP, Info, Move), configurable default mode, and top-app-bar add/import actions. Sorting (Last Used / A->Z) sits beside the search bar. Edit and delete now live in each host card's overflow menu instead of a screen-wide edit mode. Hosts screen accessible from drawer; cards show OS icon (left), host/group labels, last-used timestamp, and large action buttons underneath.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:28:- Quick Connect panel: drawer shortcut opens a bottom sheet with Host/IP, Port (default 22), Username, auth selector (password, identity, both), Mosh toggle, forwarded-port dropdown, and optional script picker; includes "Connect" + "Pin to Favorites" buttons and retains last-used values for fast reuse.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:29:- New Connection sheet: Basic section (Name, Host, Port, Username). Authentication fields sit directly under Username and include auth mode, password controls, and identity selection, with support for password, identity, or both. The remainder of the form covers group, notes, forwarded port selection, optional shell script, Mosh, and background behavior. No local shell or Telnet modes.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:30:- Port forwards manager: Local (`-L`) forwards only; each forward has bind/destination fields, host association (auto-start when that host connects), manual enable toggle, favorite icon, and edit/delete controls. Session toolbar shows active forward indicator for toggling on/off mid-session with toast notifications on success/failure.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:31:- Per-connection Info panel: "Info" button runs editable command presets (default: `uname -a`, `uptime`, `who`, `free -h`, `df -h` in that order) to show host diagnostics; presets configurable per connection.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:32:- Info panel editor: accessible from connection settings or within the Info panel via "Edit Commands," presenting ordered list of shell snippets (fields for label + command). Users can add/remove/reorder commands, toggle "Run automatically" vs "Run on demand," and preview output formatting.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:33:- Snippet manager: global library of reusable commands/scripts grouped by tags; snippets can insert variables (e.g., `{host}`, `{user}`), be pinned to hosts/groups for auto-run, shared via QR/export, and triggered from the session toolbar.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:35:- QR export/import flows: Move icon triggers single-host share via QR; "Export All" (hosts, keys metadata, settings) splits into multi-frame codes with checksum/resume support. Scanner UI shows frame counter, checksum indicator, and pause/resume controls.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:36:- Default keyboard row: ESC, TAB, CTRL, HOME, END, `/`, `-`, `_`, sticky swipe-to-arrow toggle (always available; control only switches sticky vs one-shot), swipe-to-scroll toggle, and Keyboard Editor shortcut. Users can replace/extend this via the Keyboard Editor pane, which provides a tiered key palette (modifiers, navigation toggles, plus dropdown families Fn, lowercase letters, uppercase letters, numbers, symbols). Drag & drop behaves like a freeform canvas per row-keys can be positioned anywhere without grid constraints; up to three rows (default single row, "+" adds more), plus optional compact-key mode.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:38:- Background session control: global setting ("Run shells in background") determines whether SSH/Mosh sessions can continue running when the app is backgrounded; per-connection override available.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:39:- Security lock: Settings include "Require biometric unlock after X minutes of inactivity" (configurable timeout, immediate lock option). App returns to lock screen when triggered, protecting identities and sessions.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:42:- Offline QR sync: encode connection/credential bundles (serialized via Kotlinx JSON, minified + Deflate-compressed before encryption) into QR frames with multi-frame playback plus LAN discovery option.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:43:- Port forwarding manager focused on Local (`-L`) forwards for Play Store-safe behavior: define forwards globally, attach them to hosts for auto-activation, and toggle them per session (with notifications on success/failure).
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:44:- Automation snippets/macros triggered on connect; quick buttons for frequently used commands.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:45:- Monitoring widgets/tiles (e.g., home screen quick connect).
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:46:- Biometric + hardware key (Passkey/YubiKey via NFC/BLE) unlock for key usage.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:58:- **Domain**: Kotlin coroutines + Flow, use case classes orchestrating SSH actions, connection state, and preferences.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:62:- Utilize `sshj` or `JSCH` fork for SSH; evaluate open-source terminal emulator (e.g., `blink-caret/term`) for rendering; wrap in Kotlin module with state reducers.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:63:- Mosh support through native library compiled with NDK (Phase 2).
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:64:- File transfers via SFTP module (sshj) powering dual-pane explorer plus SCP helper for single-shot transfers.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:68:- Zero-knowledge QR/LAN sync: secrets encrypted locally before encoding/transit; metadata minimized.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:70:- Automated security testing: static analysis (Detekt, Android Lint), dependency scanning (OWASP Dependency-Check or Gradle plugins).
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:74:- No backend required at launch; QR sync keeps everything local/offline.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:80:- **Connection List**: grouped card rows with OS/distro icons (default desktop until OS detected), environment labels, status indicators, large icon buttons with text labels (SSH, SFTP, SCP, Info, Move) along the bottom, and per-item overflow menus for edit/delete. Default sorting remains "Last Used" with alphabetical toggle. Info panel uses a modal showing command list, outputs, and Edit Commands entry point.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:81:- **Info Panel Editor**: sheet with reorderable list of command cards (label + shell snippet + auto-run toggle). Includes buttons for Add Command, Reset to defaults, and Preview output; drag handles for ordering and delete icons for removal.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:82:- **Quick Connect Sheet**: bottom sheet with Host/IP, Port, Username, auth toggle (password/identity/both), forwarded-port selector, optional script picker, and Mosh toggle; includes sticky "last used" chip row and buttons for Connect + Pin to Favorites.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:83:- **Terminal Screen**: edge-to-edge black canvas, custom toolbar (latency, toggle keep-awake, keyboard row, background toggle reflecting the global "Run shells in background" setting with per-connection overrides) with default row (ESC, TAB, CTRL, HOME, END, `/`, `-`, `_`, sticky swipe-to-arrow toggle, swipe-to-scroll toggle, Keyboard Editor shortcut). Users can redesign via the Keyboard Editor (drag from palette, add rows, compact mode). Gestures: pinch zoom, two-finger pan, hold for selection.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:85:- **Theming**: Material You dynamic colors + curated Carbon Black (`#191919`) + Blazing Flame (`#F15025`) palette distinct from JuiceSSH's black/yellow, emphasizing high-contrast terminals; typography optimized for monospace readability; accessibility guidelines (min 4.5:1 contrast, adjustable font scaling).
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:90:1. **Phase 0 - Research & Technical Spikes (Month 0-1)**: Evaluate SSH libraries, prototype terminal rendering, confirm key storage approach, survey competitors.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:91:2. **Phase 1 - Core MVP Build (Month 1-4)**: Implement architecture skeleton, connection manager, SSH sessions, keyboard, settings. Internal dogfooding.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:92:3. **Phase 2 - Beta & QR Polish (Month 4-6)**: Add QR sync flow, port forwarding, automation, analytics, polish UX, prepare Play Store listing, closed beta.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:93:4. **Phase 3 - Public Launch (Month 6-8)**: Harden security, finalize marketing site, documentation, open beta -> production rollout.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:94:5. **Phase 4 - Post-Launch (ongoing)**: Iterate on Mosh and enterprise-requested features; explore optional donations/supporter perks without gating functionality.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:97:- Decide licensing of 3rd-party SSH/terminal libs (compatibility with Play Store + monetization).
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:100:- Begin brand asset production (logos, palette) for SSHPeaches.
docs/user-wiki/developer/Product-Blueprint.md:101:- Define QR sync technical approach (chunking, encryption, error correction) and add to spike list.

Source support is broad, but some developer-blueprint extras should remain “design/inferred” unless a concrete implementation path was observed.

codex
Source verification shows SFTP/SCP and local port forwarding are implemented in `SessionService.kt`, while persistence is Room-backed and QR/security paths are concrete. Mosh also has implementation and bundled assets, but because the root README does not list it in the short “What You Can Do” list, I’ll keep it separate from README-documented SSH features as requested.
